# Decisions

## Block 1 (after 0 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 0 used / 250 total; 250 remaining.
**Question:** Does a chemistry prior work as a ranking? The prior is: low-viscosity solvent (MTBE, acetonitrile, alkanes, acetone, esters, ethers) at a high mole fraction, paired with an alcohol, combined by the log-ideal mixing rule. If it works, which solvent families and partners give the lowest measured viscosity?
**Mode:** both. Every probe is a top-prior candidate, so each one can also become the best point.
**Gain:** Expected values are about 0.4–0.6 cP (prior only). The block also gives 24 labels spread over about 20 low-viscosity molecule families. These labels will fit a structured model for the next block: ln eta = x1*a(m1) + x2*a(m2) + x1*x2*b. Molecule effects come from a prior plus learned offsets, and pair interactions are shrunk.
**Evidence:** No measurements yet. The pool has 5595 candidates covering 805 distinct pairs of 273 molecules. Each candidate is identified by 2×~170 RDKit descriptors plus mole fractions. Most pairs contain an aliphatic alcohol: butanols, propanols, pentanols, octanol and decanol are the most frequent. No pair has two low-viscosity components. I guessed some molecule identities from descriptors (MW, H-donors, TPSA, rings, logP), for example MTBE, acetonitrile, hexane, heptane, pentane, acetone, methyl acetate/ethyl formate, butanone, THF, cyclopentane and toluene. From those identities I assigned textbook pure-component viscosities as a prior (work/prior2.py), with a heuristic for unidentified molecules. Assumptions: the identities are only guesses, mixing nonideality is unknown, and alcohol plus nonpolar mixtures often deviate negatively.
**Decision:** Experiment/exploit with fixed_points. I take the lowest-prior mole fraction of 24 distinct top-ranked pairs, covering about 20 different low-viscosity "solvent" molecules. Hypothesis: measured ln y correlates with the prior (Spearman > 0.5), and the MTBE, acetonitrile, alkane and acetone mixtures are lowest. If they correlate, use a prior-mean structured model and exploit the best families. If not, rely more on data-driven molecule effects and broaden exploration.
**Hyperparameters:** Not applicable (fixed list of candidate indices 4072, 4109, 280, 242, 2915, 1298, 1414, 3774, 3078, 5046, 153, 4991, 849, 1740, 1409, 402, 1837, 3522, 660, 3171, 4269, 3233, 4867, 3357).
**Block length:** 24 measurements (about 10% of budget). That is enough families to calibrate the prior and fit molecule offsets before switching to a model-guided optimizer.
**Review:** Check the prior-vs-measured correlation, which families are lowest, and whether the deviations from log-ideal mixing are systematic. Then build the custom structured BO optimizer (work/) and check it offline.

Proposal: `{"script": "lib/fixed_points.py", "label": "prior_probe", "args": {"seed": 0, "points": [4072, 4109, 280, 242, 2915, 1298, 1414, 3774, 3078, 5046, 153, 4991, 849, 1740, 1409, 402, 1837, 3522, 660, 3171, 4269, 3233, 4867, 3357]}, "n_points": 24, "state": "restart", "warm_start": false}`


Runner: Block finished: 24 paid measurements; 226 remain.

## Block 2 (after 24 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 24 used / 250 total; 226 remaining.
**Question:** Can a structured mixture model fitted to the probe data find pairs or fractions below the current best of 0.448 cP faster than the prior alone? It also has to learn which molecules and pair interactions deviate from the guessed values.
**Mode:** both. EI exploits the predicted-low candidates and explores uncertain low-viscosity families.
**Gain:** Expect a few points in the 0.40–0.45 cP range, for example MTBE+butanol at a higher MTBE fraction or new aromatic/ester families. The block also gives labels that pin down molecule offsets and pair interactions for the next decision.
**Evidence:** Block 1 measured 24 top-prior candidates; the values ranged from 0.448 to 1.057 cP. Best: cand 4072 (hexanol + MTBE, x_MTBE=0.85) at 0.448. Next: cand 4109 (pentanol + MTBE) at 0.465, cand 3233 (propanol + pentane at x=0.52) at 0.484, cand 242 (acetonitrile + heptanol) at 0.487. On level, the prior (log-ideal mixing of guessed pure viscosities) did well: the mean ln-residual was +0.13 with SD 0.23. As a ranking it did poorly: Spearman 0.18, p=0.4, because the prior range is narrow. One outlier: acetonitrile + mol 137 gave 1.057 against a predicted 0.43, so mol 137's identity is probably wrong. In the pool the low-viscosity components never exceed a mole fraction of about 0.85–0.95, so the floor is probably around 0.3–0.4 cP. Domain knowledge used: pure-viscosity guesses from inferred identities, and alcohol + nonpolar mixtures often deviating negatively (pentane + propanol was 0.48 against a predicted 0.62, consistent with that).
**Decision:** Switch to a custom structured Bayesian linear model, work/structbo.py, warm-started with all 24 points. Model: ln y = prior + g + x1·d[m1] + x2·d[m2] + x1·x2·(c[m1]+c[m2]+e[pair]). Priors are Gaussian, the posterior is exact, and three scale hyperparameters are chosen each step by marginal likelihood over a grid. Acquisition is EI on ln y over unused pool candidates. Offline LOO RMSE was 0.257, against 0.265 for the prior alone, which is only a small gain. The model's main value is that it shares information across fractions of the same pair and across pairs that share a molecule, which the 350-D descriptor GP cannot do with 24 points.
**Hyperparameters:** d-SD is 0.35 for molecules with guessed identities and 0.8 for the rest, scaled by kd in {0.5, 1, 1.5}. s_c is in {0.2, 0.5, 1, 2} and s_e in {0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 1} (current ML choice: 0.5, 0.5, 1.0). s_g=0.3, noise=0.05 (deterministic objective; the nugget absorbs model misfit). EI with xi=0. Seed 0.
**Block length:** 20 measurements. That is enough to see whether EI finds sub-0.448 points and whether the model's predictions calibrate (z-scores), before tuning acquisition or the model (e.g., adding a descriptor kernel for unknown molecules).
**Review:** Did the best improve? Compare predicted with measured values for the 20 points (bias, z SD). Check whether se stays at the grid edge, which would mean pair-specific effects dominate and the grid should widen. Check which families the low points belong to, and whether to switch to pure exploitation of the best pairs' other fractions.

Runner: Block finished: 20 paid measurements; 206 remain.

## Block 3 (after 44 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 44 used / 250 total; 206 remaining.
**Question:** The structured model treats every molecule's offset as independent. That made block 2 spend 4 points on four near-identical C8 aromatics (42, 58, 62, 84 + 144) and gave no improvement. Can a model that shares information between chemically similar molecules find the few pool candidates that could still beat 0.448?
**Mode:** both (EI exploitation with model-driven exploration of uncertain families).
**Gain:** The model gives a small chance of improvement per point. The summed improvement probability over the pool is about 3.7, with only 3 candidates above PI 0.1, and the model is somewhat overconfident (z SD 1.37). A realistic gain is a few points in the 0.42–0.45 range. Each miss still trims families through the similarity kernel.
**Evidence:** Block 2 (structbo EI, 20 points) did not improve. Its best was 0.494, and the incumbent is still 0.4484 (cand 4072: hexanol + MTBE, x_MTBE = 0.85). The prior Spearman correlation is now 0.48 over 44 points. The mean ln residual is +0.20 (measured above log-ideal), except alcohol+pentane at -0.25. The pool rarely has a low-viscosity component above x = 0.85, and the top-fraction candidates of most low-viscosity solvents are already measured. Offline on the 44 points, adding a descriptor-similarity kernel (10-PC embedding of the RDKit descriptors, RBF) to the molecule offsets d and excess terms c helped. LOO rmse in ln y fell from 0.278 to 0.234, the log marginal likelihood rose from 35.4 to 44, and the block1→block2 forward rmse fell from 0.319 to 0.296. The ML choice is w = 0.9, l = 0.4. Domain prior used: guessed pure-component viscosities and log-ideal mixing as the prior mean.
**Decision:** Switch to work/structbo2.py (structbo plus a molecular similarity kernel), warm-started with all 44 points, using EI on ln y over unused pool candidates. The structure stays the same: ln y = prior + g + x1 d1 + x2 d2 + x1x2(c1 + c2 + e_pair).
**Hyperparameters:** ML grid each step over kd {0.5, 1, 1.5}, s_c {0.2, 0.5, 1}, s_e {0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 1}, w {0.5, 0.8, 0.9}, l {0.25, 0.4, 0.6}. I capped w at 0.9 because w = 0.95 gave z SD 1.45, which is overconfident. Also: s_g = 0.3, noise = 0.05, xi = 0, seed 0, dsd 0.35 for guessed molecules and 0.8 for the rest.
**Block length:** 25 measurements. That is enough to see whether the similarity model's EI finds improvements or just keeps discarding families, and short enough to re-check calibration and switch to TS or pure exploitation.
**Review:** Did the best fall below 0.448? Compare predicted with measured values on the 25 points (rmse, z). Check which families EI visited and whether it still repeats similar molecules. If there is still no gain, consider Thompson sampling for diversity, or targeted high-fraction checks of low-viscosity unknowns.

Runner: Block finished: 25 paid measurements; 181 remain.

## Block 4 (after 69 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 69 used / 250 total; 181 remaining.
**Question:** Does any candidate in the plausible low-viscosity part of the pool beat 0.4344? That part is about 190 unmeasured mixtures where a component with η ≤ 0.45 cP makes up at least 55% of the mixture. Is diethylamine (mol 55) or propylamine (mol 153) a low-viscosity component the prior missed?
**Mode:** exploit, with some targeted exploration.
**Gain:** Block 3 (structbo2, unrestricted EI) improved the best value from 0.4484 to 0.4344 (cand 1352: butanone 0.83 + mol 97). It also spent about 8 of its 25 points on high-uncertainty mixtures dominated by alcohols, glycols or unknown molecules (y = 1.9 to 5.9). Those cannot plausibly reach 0.43 cP. Limiting EI to the plausible subset puts every point where it could improve. Expected outcome: a small gain (sum PI about 0.8 over the subset), or confirmation that the pool optimum is near 0.43.
**Evidence:** I have 69 measurements. Every result below 0.55 is a mixture with at least 60–85% of a solvent whose pure viscosity is ≤ 0.42 cP (MTBE, butanone, acetonitrile, pentane, heptane). Alcohols diluted in MTBE or butanone show a strong negative deviation, for example propanol 0.40 + MTBE gave 0.477 against an ideal-mixing prior of 0.61.

The structbo2 model's leave-one-out (LOO) rmse in the low region is 0.21 in ln y, against 0.24 for the prior, so it is calibrated but only weakly predictive. Its lowest predicted unmeasured value is 0.485. The pool has no pair of two low-viscosity components. Low-solvent fractions above 0.85 are almost all measured already.

Domain knowledge used (as hypotheses):
- mol 55 (C4H11N, secondary amine) is diethylamine-like, about 0.32 cP.
- mol 153 (C3H9N, primary amine) is propylamine-like, about 0.36 cP.
The prior had 0.84 for both. In 3400, 77% mol 153 + 23% mol 97 gave 0.526, which fits mol 153 being low-viscosity.
**Decision:** Switch to structbo3. It is the same structured Bayesian model as structbo2: per-molecule effects, a molecular-similarity kernel and pair interaction terms, with hyperparameters chosen by marginal likelihood. Two changes:
- an `allowed` subset: candidates whose lowest-η component (domain estimate) has η ≤ 0.45 with mole fraction ≥ 0.55, plus any with model prediction < 0.65. That is 224 candidates, 189 unmeasured. If the subset runs out, the method falls back to the whole pool.
- the prior pure-viscosity guesses for mols 55 and 153 are set to 0.32 and 0.36 (prior sd 0.5).

The model is warm-started on all 69 points and refits after every measurement. EI first tests the mol 55 mixtures (55+105 at x=0.80, 55+107 at x=0.74, PI about 0.2 each):
- If they come in near 0.45–0.5, the amine hypothesis holds and the remaining amine mixtures are worth exploiting.
- If they come in high (amine + alcohol positive deviation), the model drops them quickly.
**Hyperparameters:** These are inherited from structbo2: noise 0.05, s_g 0.3, w grid {0.5, 0.8, 0.9}, l grid {0.25, 0.4, 0.6}, EI with xi = 0, seed 3. The subset thresholds (0.45 cP, fraction 0.55) come from the measured pattern: nothing with a smaller low-η fraction came near 0.5. check.py passed.
**Block length:** 25 measurements, about 13% of the subset. That is enough to resolve the amine hypothesis and work through the top of the EI ranking before I re-rank with the new data.
**Review:** Did the best value improve? Check the measured values for mols 55 and 153 and whether the amine hypothesis held. Check how much of the allowed subset is left. If gains have stopped, consider widening the subset (η ≤ 0.5, fraction ≥ 0.5), or using TS for diversity within it.

## Block 5 (after 94 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 94 used / 250 total; 156 remaining.
**Question:** Is any unmeasured candidate in the plausible low-viscosity region below 0.4344? Block 4 suggested the pool optimum may already have been found.
**Mode:** exploit (systematic coverage of the plausible region, ordered by calibrated EI).
**Gain:** Small. Summed improvement probability over the region is about 1.3 once the model's sd is inflated 1.5× for calibration, and it is spread thinly (max per point about 0.08). The realistic outcome is one or two points in the 0.42–0.45 range, or confirmation that 0.434 is the floor. Because the budget (156) is about the size of the plausible set (155), ordering mainly decides which points come first.
**Evidence:** Block 4 (structbo3 EI on a 224-candidate subset, 25 points) did not improve. Its best point was 4959 (heptane 0.82 + pentanol) at 0.445, and the amine hypothesis failed: mol 55 and mol 153 mixtures gave 0.51–0.86. Forward check, training on blocks 1–3 and predicting block 4: rmse 0.25 in ln, bias +0.02, z SD 1.55 at noise 0.05, so the model is overconfident. Within the low region the model ranks poorly (Spearman 0.2). A noise of 0.15 brings z SD to about 1.1 without changing rmse, which is the basis for sscale = 1.5. The highest low-η fractions of almost all known low-viscosity solvents (MTBE, butanone, heptane, hexane, pentane, ether, MeCN) are already measured. Every result below 0.45 has 0.82–0.85 of a solvent at ≤0.42 cP plus an alcohol, which shows negative deviation (measured about 0.75× the log-ideal prior). Domain prior used: guessed pure viscosities, and alcohol + inert solvent negative deviation.
**Decision:** Adjust: switch to work/structbo4.py, which is structbo3 plus `sscale`, an sd multiplier inside EI. It is warm-started with all 94 points. The new allowed set (work/allowed4.json, 155 candidates) contains three groups: unmeasured mixtures with ≥55% of a component whose prior is ≤0.5 cP; whole-pool candidates with calibrated PI > 0.02 and pm < 0.8, such as ether 0.54 + pentanol, 98-family and 123-family mixtures, and propanol + 214/219; and any candidate with pm < 0.65. Candidates with pm ≥ 1.0 are excluded. Same model structure otherwise.
**Hyperparameters:** sscale 1.5 (from the block-4 calibration), EI with xi 0, seed 4. Unchanged from structbo3: noise 0.05, s_g 0.3, ML grid (kd, s_c, s_e, w ∈ {0.5, 0.8, 0.9}, l ∈ {0.25, 0.4, 0.6}), molprior/dsd including 55 → ln 0.32 and 153 → ln 0.36 (the measured data now overrides those). check.py passed.
**Block length:** 30 measurements. That covers the top fifth of the region by calibrated EI, after which the ranking and the region definition (thresholds, unknown solvent families) are rechecked.
**Review:** Did the best improve? Recheck calibration on block 5 and see which families produced the lowest values. If unknown low-η solvents such as 98, 123, 227 or 28 come out lower than their prior, exploit their other fractions. Otherwise continue structbo4, possibly widening the region (prior ≤ 0.55, fraction ≥ 0.5).

Runner: Block finished: 30 paid measurements; 126 remain.

## Block 6 (after 124 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 124 used / 250 total; 126 remaining.
**Question:** Two adaptive GP blocks (B4, B5; 55 points) did not beat 0.4344. Is there still an unmeasured candidate with a real chance of going below it, and how should those chances be ranked?
**Mode:** exploit
**Gain:** The model gives a summed P(y<0.4344) of about 1.8 over these 25 points. That is optimistic, since the model was fit on selected data; I expect roughly 0.5–1 improvement, each worth a few percent below 0.434. The block also checks the new additive model's ranking ahead of the next call.
**Evidence:** Block 5 (structbo4, 30 points) best 0.441 (cand 225, MeCN 0.73 + butanol); no improvement. About 8 of its points went to viscous or unknown pairs (0.8–1.4). New model work/addm2.py: ridge-regularized log-ideal mixing. Each molecule's prior ln eta gets a fitted offset, plus x(1−x) terms (global and per viscous component) and pair offsets. LOO rmse is 0.29 overall and 0.155 on points below 0.6 cP. Fit on blocks 1–4 and tested on block 5: rmse 0.195, Spearman 0.49, better than structbo's 0.25 calibration. The model puts alcohols about 0.65–0.75 ln below their prior, so they behave as much less viscous in mixtures with low-viscosity solvents. In each pair, the highest fraction of the low-viscosity component is nearly always already measured. The remaining candidates are next-fraction neighbours or untested pairs, all predicted around 0.50–0.60 cP. Prediction for each candidate = mean of (model) and (measured higher-fraction neighbour + the model's within-pair change); both estimates agree to about 0.02 ln. Domain prior used: the guessed solvent and alcohol identities, and that viscosity rises monotonically as the viscous component's fraction increases.
**Decision:** switch from structbo4 (adaptive EI) to fixed_points. The 25 points are the unmeasured candidates with the highest PI(y<0.4344) under the additive model (sd 0.17), with at most one candidate per pair to avoid correlated outcomes. Examples: 4119 MTBE 0.55 + propanol, 4083 MTBE + butanol, 5081, 1337, 1310, 260, 848, etc.
**Hyperparameters:** ridge lam_mol 0.01, lam_inter 0.1, lam_pair 1, chosen by LOO rmse on points below 0.6 cP; PI sd 0.17 (LOO 0.155, forward 0.195).
**Block length:** 25. These are the clear top PI candidates; below them PI falls under 0.03 each. After this block I refit and re-rank, keeping about 100 points for follow-up.
**Review:** Was anything below 0.434? Is block 6 calibrated (rmse, bias)? If a pair beats its prediction, measure its other fractions or partners. Otherwise re-rank by PI and allow 2 per pair.

Runner: Block finished: 25 paid measurements; 101 remain.

## Block 7 (after 149 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 149 used / 250 total; 101 remaining.
**Question:** Block 6 (25 top-PI fixed points) did not beat 0.4344. Its best was 0.513, and the model's PI predictions held up (rmse 0.096 ln, bias +0.064, Spearman 0.69). Most of the remaining chance now lies in (a) the strongest remaining near-measured candidates and (b) unmeasured low-prior molecules whose viscosity was estimated from descriptors. How should these two be traded off, adaptively?
**Mode:** exploit (with uncertainty-driven exploration of uncertain low-prior molecules)
**Gain:** This is a small chance of improvement. Summed PI over the next 100 is about 1.2 under the new hierarchical model and about 0.36 under addm2, so I expect at most about one improvement, worth a few percent. A sequential refit avoids wasting points on candidates correlated with a pair that has just turned out poor.
**Evidence:** Over 149 measurements the best is 0.4344 (cand 1352, butanone 0.83 + tert-amyl alcohol). B6 measured 0.51–0.75; the per-pair high-fraction neighbours were already measured. The refit addm2 gives max PI 0.033 per candidate. From the fitted offsets: known non-alcohol solvents have offset sd 0.11 (mean -0.28). Known alcohols have mean -0.63 and sd 0.37, the H-bond breaking on dilution. Unknown (descriptor-prior) molecules have sd 0.68 overall and about 0.3 among low-prior ones. A new hierarchical Bayesian linear model (work/hb7.py) adds group-mean offsets with these group sds. Methanol and ethanol are excluded from the alcohol group, because they cannot fall below their own 0.54/1.07 cP. In the forward tests it predicts B5 at low-y rmse 0.12 and B6 at 0.11, with z sd about 0.6, so its sd is conservative. Its top picks overlap addm2's (4118, 159, 4082, 4968, 3414, 679) and add uncertain unknown low-prior molecules (225, 226, 147, 154, 224). Domain prior used: guessed solvent and alcohol identities, the log-ideal mixing prior, and alcohol dilution lowering the mixture viscosity.
**Decision:** Switch to the custom work/hbo7.py: an exact Gaussian posterior of the hierarchical model, refit after every measurement, with EI on ln y. It is warm-started with all 149 observations. It checked OK with check.py, and an offline fantasy run took about 1 s per step.
**Hyperparameters:** group offset sds (unk, unk-alc, known, known-alc) = 0.3/0.3/0.1/0.25, from the empirical offset spread; pair sd 0.07; noise 0.12 for y<0.8 and 0.3 above (B6 rmse about 0.1, larger scatter for viscous points); acquisition scatter 0.1 (between-source scatter, e.g. 0.504 vs 0.441 at neighbouring fractions of the same pair); EI.
**Block length:** 30. That is enough to see whether the EI picks among unknown low-prior molecules pay off before I recalibrate group sds and the noise. It leaves 71 for further adaptation.
**Review:** Was there any value below 0.434? Check calibration separately for points involving unknown molecules (z-scores), and whether any unknown molecule's offset came out strongly negative; if so, exploit its other pairs and fractions. If the unknown picks were all poor, shrink the unknown sd and continue (continue hbo7 or restart with new sds).

Runner: Block finished: 30 paid measurements; 71 remain.

## Block 8 (after 179 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 179 used / 250 total; 71 remaining.
**Question:** Is the pool exhausted, or does the hierarchical model (hbo7) still have usable leads? Is the model calibrated well enough to trust?
**Mode:** exploit (with residual exploration of uncertain molecules via EI).
**Gain:** Small. The model's PI for single candidates is about 0.5–0.7% each, and the summed chance of beating 0.4344 over the block is roughly 0.1–0.2. No alternative scored higher offline.
**Evidence:** B7 (30 pts, hbo7 EI) found no improvement (best 0.539). Forward calibration of hbo7 trained on B1–6 and predicting B7: rmse 0.24 ln (outliers 3414 at 1.26 and 279 at 1.41), z mean +0.35, z sd 1.21, Spearman 0.60. The model is somewhat optimistic but ranks reasonably, so its claim that the pool is nearly exhausted is credible. Top pairs (1352, 225, 4959, 4072, 4109) already have their most favourable fraction measured. Unmeasured low-prior molecules (218, 114, 15, 149, 96, 216) only occur paired with viscous alcohols, with predicted y of 0.79 or more. LOO residuals correlate only weakly with candidate index: 0.25 at index gap ≤5, which is mostly same-pair, and about 0 beyond that. So I found no hidden source or batch structure to exploit. Offline fantasies with scat 0.15 or with PI give much the same top list (4136, 659, 1568, 2969, 277, 537, 1772, 5267...).
**Decision:** continue hbo7 with its saved state unchanged. It re-fits after each point, so any surprise (e.g. a molecule with a strong negative offset) moves the next picks to that molecule's other pairs and fractions.
**Hyperparameters:** unchanged (sdo [0.3,0.3,0.1,0.25], psd 0.07, sig_lo 0.12, sig_hi 0.3, scat 0.1, EI). The alternatives I tested offline (scat 0.15, PI) did not materially change the picks.
**Block length:** 25 measurements, leaving 46 for a final review: a last exploitation pass and any re-targeting if a lead appears.
**Review:** Any value < 0.4344? Re-check calibration (z mean/sd) on B8. If a molecule shows a strongly negative offset, focus on its remaining pairs. Otherwise, finish the budget with hbo7 (continue), possibly switching to PI near the end.

Proposal: `{"script": "work/hbo7.py", "label": "hbo7", "args": {}, "n_points": 25, "state": "continue", "warm_start": false}`


Runner: Block finished: 25 paid measurements; 46 remain.

## Block 9 (after 204 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 204 used / 250 total; 46 remaining.
**Question:** With the pool apparently exhausted, which acquisition gives the best remaining chance of beating 0.4344: EI (which now favours high-variance unknown molecules) or PI (which favours low-mean candidates)?
**Mode:** exploit.
**Gain:** Small. Under PI the model gives each pick roughly a 0.15–0.3% chance of beating 0.4344, about 5% summed over 24 picks. The model's own uncertainty is if anything too wide, so the real chance may be lower. No offline alternative scored higher.
**Evidence:** B8 (hbo7 EI, 25 pts) gave no gain; its best was 0.578 (cand 537). Forward calibration on B8, training on B1–7: rmse 0.125 ln, z mean +0.05, z sd 0.57, Spearman 0.83. The model is now well calibrated and conservative, and it predicts every unmeasured candidate at ≥0.598 cP. EI values are all about 1e-4 and essentially tied. EI's top picks have means near 1.05 with sd 0.3 (about 0.15% chance each), while PI's top picks have means 0.60–0.67 with sd 0.12–0.16 (about 0.2–0.3% each). For unknown molecules, the B7/B8 residuals were not systematically negative (z mean 0.05–0.26), so their large variance does not point to hidden low-viscosity components. Domain prior used: low-viscosity solvents (ethers, ketones, nitriles, alkanes) with alcohols. Mixtures of these that score below 0.5 are already measured at their most favourable fractions.
**Decision:** switch acquisition. Restart hbo7 (same hierarchical Bayesian linear model, same priors) with acq="pi", warm-started on all 204 observations, under a new label hbo7pi. It refits after every point, so a surprise moves the next picks to that molecule or pair.
**Hyperparameters:** identical to hbo7 (sdo [0.3,0.3,0.1,0.25], psd 0.07, sig_lo 0.12, sig_hi 0.3, scat 0.1). Only acq is changed, from ei to pi, because the goal is now only to beat the incumbent and EI's variance bonus is not supported by the calibration.
**Block length:** 24 measurements, leaving 22 for a final block that can re-target if a lead appears.
**Review:** Any value <0.4344? Check B9 calibration (cal8.py with tb [8,9]). If a pair or molecule beats its prediction by more than 2 sd, focus the final block on its neighbours, i.e. other fractions of the same pair and that molecule's other partners. Otherwise continue hbo7pi to finish the budget.

## Block 10 (after 228 of 250 measurements)

**Budget:** 228 used / 250 total; 22 remaining.
**Question:** Can any unmeasured candidate still beat 0.4344 cP (cand 1352)? With only 22 measurements left, nothing is worth learning unless it can lead to a better final value.
**Mode:** exploit (final block).
**Gain:** Small. Each top probability-of-improvement (PI) pick has PI ≈ 0.1–0.16%, so the whole block has roughly a 2–3% chance of a new best. No other method has a better case.
**Evidence:** B9 (24 hbo7 PI picks) all landed at 0.566–1.04, median 0.626. That matches the model, which predicts every unmeasured candidate at ≥0.598. The model stays calibrated (B8 forward check: rmse 0.125). The best value has not moved since B3. I re-ranked unmeasured candidates by the ideal-mixing prior (x·ln η1 + (1−x)·ln η2). The lowest expected value is 0.494 (278: MeCN + an unknown molecule). Its sibling fractions were measured at 1.06 and 1.41, so the prior is badly off for that molecule. Domain knowledge used: a value below 0.43 needs ≥85% of a solvent at ≤0.3 cP. Those pairs' favourable fractions are already measured.
**Decision:** Continue label hbo7pi: the work/hbo7.py hierarchical Bayesian linear model with PI acquisition. It refits after each measurement, so any surprise result immediately steers later picks to that pair's other fractions and that molecule's other partners. In a 25-pick simulation where each pick was assigned its predicted value, the first picks were 450, 4543, 529, 5206, 4853, 4686, 3972, and so on.
**Hyperparameters:** These are the args from B9, unchanged (work/hbo7_args.json with acq=pi). PI on ln y suits a final block whose only goal is to beat the incumbent.
**Block length:** 22, the whole remaining budget. The optimizer adapts after every point, so stopping partway to review would not add anything.
**Review:** The campaign ends after this block. Report the best value.

Proposal: `{"script": "work/hbo7.py", "label": "hbo7pi", "args": {}, "n_points": 22, "state": "continue", "warm_start": false}`
